市場疑慮拖累美股，短線聚焦 Fed 決策與財報
2026 年 9 月 15 日
一、 9/14 美股下挫、美債殖利率曲線熊市趨平，且油價大漲
1. 股票指數：
⚫ 道瓊工業指數下跌 0.29%，收 52,421.20 點；
⚫ S&P 500 指數下挫 0.48%，收 7,619.98 點；
⚫ 那斯達克綜合指數下跌 0.56%，收 26,186.41 點；
⚫ 費城半導體指數下跌 5.86%，收 11,131.28 點。
2. 當日重要個股表現：
⚫ 在科技類股方面，輝達下跌約 3.4%、美光下跌 5.3%、邁威爾下跌 7.3%；

⚫ 通訊類股方面，Netflix 上漲 3.8%、Take-Two 上漲 3.5%、Fox 上漲 4.6%；
⚫ 軟體類股方面 ，ServiceNow、Adobe、Workday 等軟體股則上漲約 4% 至
7.4%。
3. 美國債市漲跌不一、殖利率曲線走勢熊市趨平。2 年期美國公債殖利率上升 3.24 個基
點至 4.66%，10 年期殖利率上升 2.06 個基點至 4.99%，10 年期殖利率一度升破 5%，
而 30 年期殖利率則下跌約 0.76 個基點至 5.34%。短率上升，反映市場對未來美國聯
準會升息的預期。
4. 油價因中東衝突與供給風險再度升高而上漲 ，WTI 原油上漲 1.81%，收在每桶 101.86
美元，布蘭特原油則上漲 1.56%，收在 106.24 美元。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. AI 開發放緩疑慮，壓抑硬體需求預期：Anthropic 執行長 Dario Amodei 於上週末
呼籲放慢前沿 AI 能力提升速度，並提出第三方安全評估與業界協調方案，使 AI 擴

不另行通知。
張節奏成為新的不確定因素。我們研判，半導體位於 AI 建置支出的上游，股價對新
增算力投資的成長速度尤其敏感。即使既有訂單未立即改變，只要市場降低對未來數
季增速的期待，GPU、高頻寬記憶體及網通設備供應鏈的評價便可能先行調整；目前
仍須區分投資預期降溫與實際訂單下修。
2. 中東能源設施遇襲，油價推升通膨風險：沙烏地阿拉伯能源設施及中東船舶遇襲，引
發供應疑慮，布蘭特原油期貨 9 月 14 日結算價升至每桶 106.24 美元。油價持續偏
高可能透過運輸與生產成本以及民眾實質購買力影響企業獲利；若進一步傳導至通膨

預期，將延長貨幣政策維持緊縮的時間，對股市評價形成壓力。
3. 十年美債殖利率一度突破 5%，評價與融資承壓：美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中突破
5%，為 2023 年以來首見。殖利率上升提高未來獲利的折現率，對依賴長期成長預
期支撐評價的股票較不利。另一方面，資料中心若仰賴借款擴張，融資成本上升也會
提高投資報酬門檻，可能影響新增建置計畫，形成 AI 供應鏈面臨的另一項壓力。
4. 通膨月增回升 ， 升息前觀望氣氛加重 ：BLS 於 9 月 11 日公布 ，8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%
，高於 7 月的 0.1%，核心 CPI 月增率也由 0.2% 升至 0.3%。我們認為，短期物
價動能回升，加上能源價格偏高，提高聯準會維持緊縮政策的壓力。投資人除了評估
當次升息，也須重新衡量後續利率高點與維持時間，增加 FOMC 會議前高波動股票
的調整壓力。
5. 成長與評價雙重重估，放大半導體跌幅：本波股市回檔壓力同時來自獲利成長預期與
市場願給的評價倍數。AI 開發放緩疑慮影響未來營收想像，殖利率上行則壓低未來獲

利的現值。當股價已反映較高成長期待，只要新增訊息不如預期，即使當期營收仍強
勁，也可能出現明顯修正。後續應觀察獲利上修能否抵銷評價收縮，不能僅以跌幅大
就認定股價便宜。

三、市場看法
1. 龍頭財報仍具成長，支持中期正向看法：輝達 8 月 26 日公布的 2027 會計年度第
二季資料中心營收達 890 億美元、年增 117%，並預估第三季總營收 1,080 億美元
、上下浮動 2%。博通 9 月 2 日公布的 2026 會計年度第三季 AI 半導體營收為
167 億美元、年增 221%，並預估第四季達 217 億美元。上述實績與公司展望，是
我們對 AI 核心供應鏈維持中期正向看法的依據；惟這些財測發布於本次事件之前，
仍須追蹤後續更新。
2. 放緩方案尚待落地，需求影響須分項評估：Anthropic 執行長 Amodei 明確表示，放

緩不代表停止模型訓練，但也提出研議訓練算力等限制，因此實際影響取決於後續執
行方式。既有模型的商業使用、推論與安全測試仍可能帶來算力需求，惟能否抵銷前
沿訓練投資的放緩，必須由使用量、訂單與收入驗證。此時適合保留成長觀點，同時
提高對支出變化的敏感度。
3. 科技股仍有輪動，軟體與半導體強弱分明：9/14 當日企業軟體與資安股逆勢上漲，顯
示科技股內部仍有輪動與受惠程度的差異。我們認為，若前沿 AI 開發放緩，部分既
有軟體業者面臨的競爭及產品替代壓力可能減輕；AI 安全疑慮升高，也可能強化資安
、權限控管及企業治理需求。相較之下，半導體更直接承受新增建置支出預期變化。
因此，9/14 當日費半重挫尚不足以推論整體科技需求同步轉弱；後續宜比較訂單能見
度、訂閱收入、獲利與現金流，單日相對強勢仍須由基本面延續性驗證。
4. 升息機率約九成，會後政策態度才是關鍵：依 Reuters 於美東 9 月 14 日收盤報導
引述的 CME FedWatch 資料，本次升息一碼的機率約 90%。若美東 9 月 16 日

FOMC 確實升息 0.25 個百分點，利率區間將升至 3.75% 至 4.00%，當次升息已
大致反映於市場預期。我們認為，會後聲明及主席記者會對未來利率政策的態度才是
關鍵：是否暗示還需升息、利率須維持高檔多久，以及是否擔心能源漲價向核心通膨

擴散，並須搭配點陣圖判讀。若後續緊縮訊號未超出預期、長債殖利率趨穩，有利評
價修復；若利率路徑較預期更高、更持久，科技股仍可能承壓。
5. 通膨改善仍不均衡，避免過早判定轉折：8 月核心 CPI 年增率為 2.4%，低於 7 月
的 2.5%，但其月增率由 0.2% 升至 0.3%，顯示年增放緩與短期動能回升並存。後
續須觀察能源漲價是否向服務價格與通膨預期擴散。若油價回穩、核心物價再度降溫
，利率壓力可望緩和；若能源衝擊持續，股市調整時間可能拉長。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦 Fed 決策與 10 月美股財報，科技股中期向上趨勢料不致有變：我們目前仍

維持 AI 核心供應鏈中期正向看法，較看好能將營收成長轉化為獲利與現金流的企業
，但宜密切觀察本週的 FOMC 會議結果與 10 月中將起跑的美股財報。若雲端業者維
持資本支出、晶片公司財測穩定，且 FOMC 此次會議未釋出超預期緊縮訊號，有利
市場信心修復；然而，若出現廣泛支出下修、訂單遞延，或油價與殖利率持續上行，
則應拉長觀察期並重新檢視通膨與利率對於股市動能前景的影響。
2. 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：目前美國的通膨數據仍具韌性，且就業市場
穩健，使年底前市場升息預期再度升溫。市場對美國財政赤字、公債供給及長期通膨
仍要求較高風險補償，30 年期殖利率與短天期利差仍維持高檔，顯示期限溢酬尚未收
斂，考量當前 3～10 年期美國公債與投資級債能鎖定目前較高息收，且其受長端財政
風險影響也相對較小，故現階段3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對
較佳。

美国 9 月 CPI：风险在 油价的 Higher for

《 9 月美国 CPI 前瞻： 核心环比预计

报 20261011》 
2026-10-11

[Table_Tag]    [Table_Summary] 
◼ 核心观点：9 月美国 CPI 全面超预期，核心项目是超预期的主要来源。
与 8 月少数项目的扰动相比，9 月核心商品与服务环比的反弹具有广泛
性，通胀扩散度显著走高，超级核心通胀 3 个月均年率升至 4%以上的
高位，且二手车、 房租等分项近期走势与领先指数 “脱钩” 。这一通胀黏
性背后的必要条件是美国经济增长的韧性超预期，充分条件则是油价
“Higher for Longer”的现实强化了通胀的传导。展望看，若油价持续在
高位反复， 则高油价给明年通胀带来的基数效应利好的意义将减弱。货
币政策方面，10 月 28 日美联储加息与否对 11 月 3 日的美国中期选举
尤其重要， 因此虽然地缘事件的不确定性很高， 但我们更担忧伊朗在 10
月下旬升级冲突、给特朗普施压的可能性，这将强化美联储 10 月加息
预期走高的风险。就策略而言，中期选举仍是一个重要的事件节点，中
选后各方暂时消停、 美国国会格局清晰，市场的不确定性才会消除；我
们维持此前观点：10 月的不确定性高， 对风险偏好构成持续压制， 更适
合 11 月 3 日中选结果公布后进行右侧交易。 
◼ 美国 9 月 CPI：全面超预期， 加息预期升温。美国 9 月 CPI 环比+0.65%，
预期+0.6%， 前值+0.4%； 核心CPI 环比+0.33%， 预期+0.2%， 前值+0.29%。
CPI 同比+3.74%，预期+3.6%，前值+3.40%；核心 CPI 同比+2.56%，预
期+2.5%，前值+2.45%。由于总体 CPI 环比基本符合预期，核心项目的
超预期是 9 月通胀全面超预期的主要驱动，且核心 CPI 环比是在 8 月
“异常”的高基数上延续高于预期，带动核心 CPI 环比 3 个月均值显
著走高。 数据发布后，紧货币交易强化，交易员加息定价10 月 28 日美
联储加息概率从 20%左右升至最高 50%以上，全年加息预期最高升至约
1.5 次，美股和黄金下跌，美债收益率和美元上涨。随后，数位美联储
官员发言仍释放鸽派信号，表示不急于加息，10 月加息预期回落至约
30%，美债利率回落。 
◼ 通胀结构：核心分项环比全面走高，通胀广度扩散。总体来看，核心商
品、 居住服务、 除居住核心服务等核心CPI 大类在 9 月均环比反弹， 显
示通胀黏性仍大。①核心商品方面，9 月核心商品 CPI 环比+0.25%，为
2025 年 2 月以来最高， 前值+0.11%；结构上， 全球供应链压力、 关税和
AI 相关商品涨价均是贡献来源。其中，高占比的二手车价格拉动 9.6bps，
是核心商品环比上涨的主要贡献，且与其领先指标 Manheim 二手车走
势脱钩。与关税相关的休闲商品与服装， 二者环比分别由上月的+0.02%、
0%反弹至+0.31%、+0.86%，其中休闲商品中的玩具环比涨幅突出，拉
动核心 CPI 环比 6.6bps。②居住通胀方面，9 月居住通胀环比+0.39%，
较前值 +0.26%显著 反弹， 其 中租 金和 自住 房折算 环 比分别 反弹 至
+0.25%、+0.35%的较高中枢， 酒店住宿环比保持在+2.04%的高位。从领
先指标——领先约 16 个月的房价和领先约 6—8 个月的租金来看，居住
通胀应当仍有一定的下行空间，其在本月的反弹略超预期。③超级核心
通胀方面，由于上月的电信服务等一次性扰动的消退，9 月非居住核心
服务通胀环比由前值+0.42%回落至+0.34%， 超级核心通胀 （包括居住服
务中的酒店住宿） 环比由前值+0.51%回落至+0.42%，但环比均处于较高
中枢，且 3 个月均年率分别升至 4.43%、4.48%的高位。尤其是，从结
构来看，交运、休闲服务、酒店等更广泛的项目出现了上涨，超级核心
通胀结构所体现的通胀黏性更加突出。④通胀扩散度方面，反映通胀广
度的通胀扩散指数在 9 月显著走高，环比折年增速大于过去 5 年均值的
项目占比从 17.6%提升至 24.76%，环比折年增速大于 4%的项目占比从
20%提升至 35%。 
◼ 展望与策略启示： 风险在于油价的“higher for longer”：总体来看，9 月
美国 CPI 全面超预期， 其中核心项目是超预期的主要来源。 与8 月少数
项目的扰动相比，9 月核心商品与核心服务环比的反弹具有广泛性，通
胀扩散度显著走高， 我们认为其背后的必要条件是美国经济增长的韧性

2 / 7 
超预期，充分条件则是油价“Higher for Longer”的现实强化了通胀的
传导， 这对美联储来说是一个不容乐观的信号。展望看，①通胀趋势方
面，若油价持续在高位反复，则我们此前看好的“高油价带来的基数效
应”意义减弱：一来是油价的回落幅度有限， 产生的基数效应更加有限；
二来是高油价的持续意味着通胀更大的扩散风险。②货币政策方面，对
于特朗普而言，10 月 28 日美联储加息与否，对 11 月 3 日的美国中期
选举尤其重要，因此，虽然地缘事件的不确定性很高，但我们更担忧上
行风险，即伊朗在中期选举前的 10 月下旬主动挑高油价、给特朗普政
府施压的可能性，这将强化美联储 10 月加息预期走高的风险。就策略
启示而言， 我们强调在此前报告中的观点， 美国中期选举仍然是一个重
要的事件节点，中选后美伊双方暂时消停、美国国会格局清晰， 市场的
不确定性才会逐步消除；10 月的不确定性高，对风险偏好构成持续压
制，因此更适合 11 月 3 日中选结果公布后进行右侧交易。 
◼ 风险提示：油价失控推高通胀； 美联储维持高利率水平时间过长， 引发
金融系统流动性危机。

东吴证券投资评级标准 
投资评级基于分析师对报告发布日后 6 至 12 个月内行业或公司回报潜力相对基准表现
的预期（A 股市场基准为沪深 300 指数，香港市场基准为恒生指数，美国市场基准为标普 
500 指数， 新三板基准指数为三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转
让标的），北交所基准指数为北证 50 指数），具体如下： 
公司投资评级： 
买入：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在 15%以上； 
增持：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于 5%与 15%之间； 
中性：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-15%与-5%之间； 
卖出：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在-15%以下。 
行业投资评级： 
增持： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对强于基准 5%以上； 
中性： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对基准-5%与 5%； 
减持： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对弱于基准 5%以上。 
我们在此提醒您， 不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。 我们采用的是相
对评级体系， 表示投资的相对比重建议。 投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定应当充分考虑自身
特定状况， 如具体投资目的、 财务状况以及特定需求等， 并完整理解和使用本报告内容， 不
应视本报告为做出投资决策的唯一因素。

### Page 1

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
Hermes Testing Solutions
Shifting-left, scaling-up: dual catalysts in chip
probing and CPO
Jack-of-all-trades for bespoke testing solutions in the semiconductor industry
We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 PT. HTSI is
uniquely positioned to benefit from two key semiconductor testing trends: shift-left
testing and the emerging CPO testing market. Leveraging its long-standing relationship

with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across electrical test engineering,
customised test equipment, thermal control modules, probe cards, and CPO testing
solutions. We forecast revenue growth of 127%/152%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, driven
by increasing adoption of thermal control modules for wafer-level test and initial
deployment of CPO insertion 2 and insertion 3 test equipment through ficonTEC.
Additional 2028E growth drivers include reflow tools, laser clean solutions, and MEMS

probe cards. The stock trades at 27/15x 2027/28E PE, versus ~35x NTM PE for handler-
equipment peers, offering re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified,
and its CPO presence expands. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Shift-left beneficiary with exceptional thermal control modules
Thermal control modules should be HTSI's largest growth driver over the next several
years. We expect increasing chip power consumption, rising package complexity, and

tighter probe pitches to drive adoption of advanced thermal control solutions used
alongside leading-edge probers, in additional to more wafer level insertions and longer
test time. We estimate HTSI will ship approximately 3,000 thermal-control modules in
2027E, up from around 1,200 units in 2026E, sales contribution rise from ~30% in
Q226 to >50% of sales by 2027E. The launch of its second-generation platform in
2027E, which carries 25-50% higher ASPs, should further improve profitability. We

forecast GM expansion from 55.5% in 2026E to 58.3%/60.0% in 2027E/28E.
Unique position to capture CPO upside
Through its partnership with German optical-automation leader ficonTEC, HTSI is
positioned to participate in the initial ramp of CPO testing equipment. HTSI supports
local manufacturing, installation, automation and after-sales service, making it a key
enabler of volume production. We estimate HTSI captures approximately 30% of
equipment value across the 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems we forecast for

delivery in 2027E, with further potential upside in 2028E as adoption expands.
Valuation: initiate coverage with a Buy rating and price target NT$8,880
We forecast EPS of NT$29.1 in 2026E (including a one-off employee share purchase
programme impact), rising to NT$153.3/286.5 in 2027E/28E. Our NT$8,880 price target
is based on 31x average 2028E PE, versus handler-equipment peers forward PE at
roughly 35x, reflecting HTSI's unique exposure to both shift-left testing, CPO, and our

estimate of 32% LT earnings CAGR. We initiate with a Buy rating.

### Page 2

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 2
Hermes Testing Solutions UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will HTSI be able to keep its moat within chip probing thermal control modules?
Yes. We expect HTSI's competitive position to strengthen as chip power consumption, package
complexity, and probe density continue to increase. The company is developing next-generation

thermal control solutions, including liquid cooling technologies, while also expanding into specialized
chuck solutions designed to address wafer warpage. In addition, HTSI's close collaboration with
TSMC, TEL, Advantest, and other OSATs should reinforce its position within advanced wafer-level
testing.
Q: Will insertion 2 of CPO testing process be skipped?
No. We believe concerns surrounding insertion 2 adoption are overdone. While some industry

participants favor bypassing insertion 2 due to throughput and ROI considerations, doing so increases
the risk of defective optical engines being identified only after the components are delivered to
OSATs, creating yield accountability challenges and higher packaging losses. Based on our industry
studies, we expect insertion 2 testing to remain an important part of the CPO workflow over at least
the next two years as the industry focuses on improving PIC yields and overall testing efficiency.

We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 price target. HTSI
offers unique exposure to two of the semiconductor testing industry's most attractive growth drivers:
1) shift-left testing and 2) CPO testing. The industry's shift-left testing strategy will have more test
insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in addition to longer CP time. Leveraging its
longstanding relationship with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across thermal control

modules, electrical-test engineering services, customized equipment, and emerging CPO testing
solutions. We believe there could be re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified, and
its CPO presence expands.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'

pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI currently trades at just 15x of our 2028E EPS versus
handler-equipment peers forward PE at roughly 35x. We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO
testing equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on
our industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E,

this should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands. Our
reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR
vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for
re-rating.

Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It focuses on providing

semiconductor testing and inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical support, and
maintenance services. Its main business segments are customized semiconductor equipment,
engineering services, and probe cards and other materials.

### Page 3

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 3
Hermes Testing Solutions UBS Research
Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 3S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.
Figure 1: Semiconductor testing process: from wafer level to package level

Source: UBS
We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

Nonetheless, leaders within the testing sector will standout among competition, with
greater sales CAGR comparing to the industry.
We see HTSI's sales for the next few years to mainly be driven by 1) thermal control
module shipment and 2) CPO equipment shipment. In the past, thermal control

modules weren't necessary for chip probing, due to less TDP for the chips. However, as
new iterations of AI accelerators now got substantial power draw, this unlocks a brand
new TAM for HTSI to enter with their thermal control modules to pair with probers, in
order to prevent probe card damages.
 Robust thermal control module shipments.  We estimate HTSI to ship
3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2027E/28E, with continuous demand in
future years, bringing their 2026-31E semiconductor equipment sales CAGR to

48%.

### Page 4

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 4
 Thermal control module ASP expansion.  Gen 2 thermal control modules will
start to ship in 2027E which will drive tool ASP up around 25-50%.
 Demand supported by Advantest expansions. We believe the 3,000 thermal
control modules shipment forecast for 2027E is not far-fetched, as we read-across
from Advantest's announced capacity expansion plan (testers will be paired with
probers with a 1:1 ratio) with increasing capacity up to 5,000 testers within

FY2027/3 and they also announced a 10,000-unit target within CY2028.
Although the testers capacity expansion plan includes testers for final test, we do
see a good portion to be allocated to chip probing given shift-left testing trend
increasing both testing insertions and testing time before chip packaging.
 CPO is another brand new TAM for semiconductor testing.  As insertion 2
and 3 testing equipment demand starts to rise in 2027 with scale-out CPO

adoption, we see HTSI to ship 40/70 insertion 2 and 3 equipment in 2027E, with
this number growing in 2028E to 80/160 and higher in future years as CPO
proliferates, and driving 52% 2026-31E test equipment & engineering services
sales CAGR.
 CPO to see content increase. Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment may start to ship in
2027E, with higher throughput and tool ASP .
 Insertion 2 might scale down, but not happening anytime soon. Insertion 2
equipment shipment might come down in 2029E and beyond, as foundries scale-

up yield for photonic IC (PIC) in order to lessen the time consuming insertion 2
testing burden.

Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe HTSI is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers and
capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see HTSI is uniquely positioned as
the main beneficiary of two of the strongest growth drivers within semiconductor
testing: testing shift-left and CPO.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing

### Page 5

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 5
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging.
Figure 4: CP is set to grow substantially with shift-left, each packaging step set to be MORE costly than the prior step

RDL / RWAT testing Wafer-level CP (WLCP)
CoWChiplet (if use SoIC) CoW
CP (Die A)
CP (Die B)
Assembly Test (AST)
oS
Source: UBS
The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while
defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed

testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million
(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall

integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of
wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level

chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

evolution

Hermes Testing (HTSI) is a key beneficiary of this trend. Leveraging its long-standing
relationship with TSMC and expertise in semiconductor test solutions, HTSI supplies

advanced thermal control modules used alongside leading-edge probers (made by
Tokyo Electron), particularly for AI applications. These thermal control modules are
required for high TDP semiconductor wafer probe in order to maintain the temperature

### Page 6

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 6
of the chuck to avoid needle burns or probe card damages. We estimate HTSI will ship
approximately 3,000 thermal-management modules in 2027E, up from 1,200 in 2026E,
supporting sales growth of 127%/152% in 2026E/27E. HTSI is also the partner for
prober installation, prober maintenance and services. In addition, HTSI provides high-
end chuck processing services, an area that should benefit from increasing package sizes

and higher wafer warpage associated with advanced packaging.
Beyond thermal control modules, HTSI also supplies consumables and customised
solutions for wafer-level testing, including probe card cleaning sheets and laser-based
probe head cleaning systems. As probing intensity increases under the shift-left testing
model, demand for cleaning and maintenance solutions should rise alongside probe
card utilisation. Currently, cleaning sheets is around 5% of sales, but we believe it could

gain traction in 2027/28E and beyond. We see additional upside from future modular
laser cleaning systems that could be integrated directly with probers, further expanding
HTSI's content opportunity within advanced wafer-level testing.
CPO testing fuelling future growth for semiconductor testing
As interconnect bottlenecks increasingly shift from the memory wall to the copper wall,
we believe co-packaged optics (CPO) adoption is inevitable over the longer term. Testing

remains one of the key bottlenecks to CPO deployment, as optical validation differs
fundamentally from the electrical testing processes that have dominated semiconductor
manufacturing for decades. As a result, CPO is creating a new testing opportunity across
the semiconductor equipment and consumables ecosystem. We have written
extensively on CPO and opportunities that it brings to the semiconductor supply chain,
see our CPO deep dive , our thoughts on CPO timing , 2026 SiPhIA conference

takeaways, and SEMICON 2026 takeaways.
Various test equipment suppliers are scrambling to come up with ideal testing
equipment across the four insertions of CPO testing, with solutions to ensure both
optical and electrical signal integrity of the CPO solutions. The four insertions are:
 Insertion 1: Electronic IC (EIC)/Photonic IC (PIC) test.  Single-side wafer test
that has dedicated probe cards to test the fabricated EIC/PIC wafers.
 Insertion 2: Optical/Electrical test. Double sided wafer test, testing the hybrid

bonded (e.g. via TSMC's COUPE) EIC and PIC wafers by an opto-electrical test that
verifies the electrical signals on the top and optical signal on the bottom of the
hybrid bonded wafers.
 Insertion 3: Optical engine (OE) test.  The OE tests are done to ensure the
quality of the OEs and with FAU (Fiber Array Unit) attached, to ensure the signal
integrity of the OEs. The testing equipment needs to be able to handle 224G per
channel data rates, requiring high unit per hour (UPH) throughput, meticulous dirt

mitigation, and maintaining a near 100% contact rating on high-speed sockets.
 Insertion 4: ASIC final test.  This final test step is established post the OEs are
packaged together with the ASIC. The complete ASIC package (now with CPO) is
now tested for opto-electrical functionalities.
As it stands, insertion 2 and insertion 3 are the two areas where there are the most
debates in the industry. Insertion 3 suppliers such as Chroma has noted the trend of

“shift-to-middle” paradigm centered on insertion 3. In high-density scale-out switches
deploying up to 32 optical engines (e.g. Nvidia’s Spectrum-X architectures), any OE
defect results in package scrap at the CoWoS level, making 100% known good die
(KGD) validation at the singulated OE stage an absolute commercial requirement.

With its 25 years of precision test equipment expertise, ficonTEC is an early mover for
CPO test equipment and has key know-how for optical alignment, which is
quintessential for optical test. HTSI, through its partnership with ficonTEC, is set to be a
major beneficiary once CPO proliferates in 2027E-28E. As the partner of ficonTEC,
Hermes Testing is the key enabler that can help ficonTEC achieve HVM for its CPO

testing equipment. As of now, our view is that both insertion 2 and insertion 3 should be
required within the next two years, despite the difficulties surrounding double-sided
wafer probing (insertion 2), as the yield for OEs are still not high enough to avoid
defective dies. Finding out known-good OEs will be crucial before the FAU coupling
process during the latter stages of CPO packaging, to prevent impeding the final
package with multiple OEs attached to the switch ASIC (e.g. 32 OEs on Nvidia's

Spectrum-X). We estimate Hermes Testing will ship 40/70 units of insertion 2/3 testing
equipment in 2027E, with potential upside to the numbers as we view Hermes Testing
will likely assist with the ramp up for Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment in 2027E. Gen 2
insertion equipment will come with better throughput and ASP .

Beyond insertions 2 and 3, HTSI is also positioned to participate in insertion 1 testing
through customised prober solutions for photonic integrated circuit (PIC) testing. We
believe these solutions could eventually be packaged as licensable intellectual property,

creating an additional long-term growth opportunity as CPO adoption broadens. While
near-term testing growth is driven by AI accelerators, CPO represents the next major
expansion cycle for semiconductor testing. We believe HTSI is uniquely positioned to
participate across multiple test insertions, providing exposure to a potentially significant
source of incremental revenue beginning in 2027E.

HTSI's unique position for new TAM of thermal control
modules and CPO test equipment
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan, as a spin-off
from Hermes-Epitek. The company provides semiconductor testing and inspection

solutions, engineering services, customized equipment, and test consumables. HTSI
maintains a global service footprint across Taiwan (Hsinchu, Tainan), Mainland China
(Suzhou), Japan (Tokyo, Kumamoto), Singapore, Malaysia, and the United States
(Arizona), enabling close support for leading semiconductor customers.
Its main business segments are structured around three pillars:
 Semiconductor equipment and customized products : HTSI utilises a dual-

track strategy of independent R&D and also equipment agency sales. They offers

### Page 9

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 9
highly customized solutions designed to address specific client pain points and
bottlenecks. Key products include: thermal control modules that pair with a 1:1
ratio with wafer-level test probers, electron beam inspection equipment, white
light interference optical inspection systems, laser probe cleaning machines, and
the SYMPHONY fluxless reflow system.
 Testing equipment engineering services:  The company operates the largest

engineering service team in Taiwan, consisting of over 340 technical professionals.
The engineering service team provides extensive equipment life-cycle support,
including installation, relocations, modification, testing, routine maintenance, and
repair services. CPO related equipment sales/services will be recognized within this
segment, as CPO are not direct HTSI products, but rather sales recognition via its
partnership with ficonTEC.
 Probe card design, manufacturing, and cleaning materials:  HTSI delivers a

comprehensive product portfolio that supports the entire probe card life-cycle. For
probe cards, they supply membrane, cantilever, and vertical probe cards. As they
are small in scale, HTSI targets niche probe card applications such as RF, 5G/6G,
millimeter wave, LCD driver, low end logics, TIA (Transimpedance Amplifier), etc. In
addition to the three existing probe technologies, HTSI also collaborates with MJC
in Japan for MEMS probe development, targeting sales in 2028E for high-end, AI/

HPC related applications. Cleaning materials is another important business within
this segment, as HTSI source and process sand paper or other materials to offer
cleaning sheets to clean the probe cards, removing oxidations, unwanted particles
and substances that accumulated on the probe cards during the testing process.
The sales resiliency in the coming years mainly stems from its robust relationship with key
industry players, especially as an engineering and service provider with TSMC for testing

solutions. This sets up a solid foundation for its customized equipment business, as they
know the specific testing bottleneck to tackle. For instance, as Hermes-Epitek
manufacturers the probers for Tokyo Electron (TEL) and HTSI helps with installation,
services, and maintenance of the probers, they understand the thermal bottlenecks that
the wafer-level test industry has been facing and can effectively come up with module
solutions to solve this.

Margins set to expand with value-added products and services
The company has demonstrated strong execution in advanced wafer-level testing, with
H126 revenue of NT$2.2bn already exceeding 90% of FY25 revenue (NT$2.4bn).
Profitability has also improved significantly, with GM increasing from 42.1% in 2025 to
54.9% in H126.
With share of value-added products and services set to rise in 2027E in sales mix, HTSI is
set to see GM and operating leverage expansion. As a niche engineering service provider

that has robust ties to leading foundries and OSATs, we believe HTSI has substantial
pricing power and estimate HTSI' GM for thermal control modules to be around 65%,
with GM for CPO equipment of around 60% as HTSI provides an engineering-service-
like business for it. We believe these factors will help the company to reach a 60% long-
term GM range.
OPM is also set to expand with customized equipment sales rise in total sales mix. If we
exclude the one-time expense to be recognized during Q326 for employee stock

benefits (we estimate around NT$1bn), YoY opex growth will be around 99% for
2027E, with downside risk if the company conducts tight cost control measures. We
forecast the company's OPM will expand from 19.5% in 2026E, to 44.3%/47.0% in
2027/28E.

Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI still trades at just 15x 2028E P/E. We
believe market expectations for 2027E CPO testing equipment shipments remain
conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our industry analysis, we
estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this should
be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3

shipment due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO
adoption expands. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM
level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for re-rating.
We believe the market is not fully reflecting three key growth drivers:
1) Shift-left testing is still underappreciated.
As advanced packaging costs rise and AI accelerators become increasingly complex,

more thorough testing is done at the wafer-level. This should drive sustained demand
for HTSI's thermal control modules, probe card consumables, and other wafer-level
testing solutions.
2) CPO revenue potential remains underestimated.
Investor expectations for 2027 CPO equipment shipments, particularly insertion 2
testing systems, remain conservative. Based on our industry studies, we forecast HTSI
will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, with further potential

upside in 2028E as CPO adoption broadens.
3) New products provide additional optionality.
Beyond its core businesses, HTSI is expanding into higher-value solutions including mass-
reflow tools, MEMS probe cards and laser cleaning systems, creating incremental
growth opportunities that we believe are largely absent from current expectations.
We value HTSI using 31x average 2028E PE, underpinned by a 32% 2027-31E earnings
CAGR. We use 2028E PE as our view is that 2028 is the year for thermal control modules

and CPO adoption grow from the initial ramping up stage to mass adoption stage. We
believe using handlers as a peer group is justifiable for a unique company like HTSI, with
thermal control modules for chip probing sharing similar characteristics as active thermal
control used for final test, in addition to Hon Precision and Chroma having exposure to
CPO test equipment. For equipment vendors, the peer group's average NTM PE post
2022 is around 35x, and the group is currently trading at around 41x NTM PE (see

What's Priced In chart).
HTSI is trading at NT$4,190 as of 6

Source: UBS estimates
Risk to the current share price is heavily skewed (2.2:1) to the upside.
UPSIDE (NT$10,000): Our upside scenario reflects stronger than expected thermal
module and CPO equipment shipment in 2027-28E, with additional upside from better
traction of probe cards and mass reflow tools, forming 157%/91% YoY sales growth in
2027/28E and even higher operating leverage with OPM of 46.3%/50.0% in 2027/28E.

This scenario implies a 34x 2028E PE, a upside driving by more equipment demand
stemming for robust shift-left test induced equipment bottleneck, alongside CPO
proliferation with scale-up CPO equipment pull-in in 2028E.
BASE (NT$8,880): Our price target of NT$8,880 is based on 31x 2028E PE. We expect an
earnings CAGR of 32% in 2027-31E. With shift-left trend for more thermal module
requirement during testing, alongside 40/70 CPO insertion 2/3 shipment. We see these

growth drivers driving YoY sales growth from a high base of 127% in 2026E to
152%/78% YoY in 2027/2028E, with OPM of 44.3%/47.0% in 2027/28E.
DOWNSIDE (NT$1,500): Our downside scenario reflects lower shipment of thermal
control modules due to less advanced prober demand with less insertion 2 and 3
equipment shipment due to bottlenecks with CPO production, alongside limited
additional growth drivers to be picked up in 2028 such as MEMS probe card and mass

reflow tools. This leads to weaker YoY sales growth of 67%/50% in 2027/28E and
operating margin of 29.3%/32.0% in 2027/28E as equipment shipment fails to scale.
This scenario implies a 14x 2028E PE, as valuation de-rate with dimmed outlook without
CPO and limited upside from thermal control module shipments.

Company Description
Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu,
Taiwan. It focuses on providing semiconductor testing and
inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical support,
and maintenance services. Its main business segments are
customized semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and
probe cards and other materials.

Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC
applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It focuses on
providing semiconductor testing and inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical
support, and maintenance services. Its main business segments are customized

semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and probe cards and other materials.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Hermes Testing based on PE methodology.
Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less

investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected front-end/CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in
Hermes Testing gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.

- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
Hermes Testing losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
Hermes Testing, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.

- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact Hermes
Testing’s margins for equipment shipments.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

9 月通胀点评

四季度通胀增速或走平   
9 月国际大宗商品价格波动对国内通胀的影响依然较大，国际油价、有色金
属价格造成明显上拉， 黄金价格则造成一定拖累；9 月 AI 产品价格上升对通
胀的影响进一步凸显，CPI 中通信工具分项同比增速连续两个月在 10%上方；
我们认为四季度 CPI 和 PPI 同比增速或在当前增速水平保持窄幅波动， 关注
国际局势、厄尔尼诺、生产端年底补库存等因素对通胀的扰动 。 
 9 月 CPI 环比增长 0.2%，同比增长 0.9%，核心 CPI 同比增长 1.0%，服务
价格同比增长 0.8%，消费品价格同比增长 1.0%。从分项同比看，同比增
速较 8 月上升的仅有交通通信，居住、教育文化娱乐和医疗保健增速持
平于 8 月，衣着、食品烟酒、生活用品及服务、其他用品和服务增速均
较 8 月有不同程度下降。 
 从环比看，9 月食品价格环比上涨 0.4%，涨幅与上月相同，低于季节性
水平 0.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.06 个百分点，服务价格由上
月上涨 0.1%转为下降 0.2%，对 CPI 环比的影响由上月上拉约 0.04 个百
分点转为下拉约 0.08 个百分点 ， 工业消费品价格影响CPI 环比上涨约 0.22
个百分点。 
 AI 产品价格带动 9 月 CPI 同比增速上行。从同比看，9 月 CPI 同比增长
0.9%，其中，食品价格影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点，汽油价格影
响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点，扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响 CPI
同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点 ；
从细项看，猪肉和鲜菜价格合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点，牛
肉和羊肉价格合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.08 个百分点 ， 鸡蛋价格影响CPI
同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点，黄金饰品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.11 个百
分点，数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响CPI
同比上涨约 0.23 个百分点。一方面，9 月 CPI 有三个特点与此前是一致
的，一是食品价格的持续拖累，二是国际能源价格和黄金价格的持续扰
动，三是内需的疲弱反映在服务价格上；另一方面，9 月也出现了新的特
点，最为明显的是 AI 产品需求带动的价格上升是 9 月 CPI 同比增速较 8
月上升的重要因素 ， 体现在通信工具价格9 月同比增长 14.3%、 交通通信
项目 9 月同比增长 4.3%，其次是以牛肉和羊肉为代表的食品价格，出现
环比走势强于季节性的趋势。我们认为四季度 CPI 同比增速或仍将维持
相对平稳的走势，但是需要开始关注 AI 产品需求对价格的推动、极端天
气对食品价格的影响、国际局势不确定性对大宗商品价格波动的影响，
以及 PPI 同比增速上升对 CPI 的外溢性影响。 
 9 月 PPI 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 5.0%，其中生产资料同比增长 6.5%，
生活资料同比下降 0.1%。PPIRM 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 6.9% 
 关注年底补库存对 PPI 的影响。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，涨幅明显高于
万得一致预期 4.3%，影响较大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业
价格，从行业看主要的上行拉动，一是石油、有色、化工等受输入性因
素影响较大的行业，二是受 AI 需求带动较大的计算机通信行业，三是受
季节性因素影响较大的行业，比如煤炭。我们认为四季度国际局势的不
确定性和国内生产端季节性补库存的具体情况 ， 都可能对PPI 价格造成影
响 ， 但考虑到目前国内供强虚弱的情况没有明显变化 ， 我们认为PPI 同比
增速短期虽然回落空间不大，但进一步上升的空间也较小 。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行的风险 ；欧美经济回落速度 偏快；国际局
势复杂化。

### Page 2

2026 年 10 月 14 日 9 月通胀点评 2

CPI 
9 月 CPI 环比增速较 8 月回落。9 月 CPI 环比增长 0.2%，增速较 8 月下降 0.2 个百分点。从分项环
比看，9 月环比上行的包括交通通信 （1.0%） 、 衣着 （0.6%） 、 食品烟酒 （0.2%） 、 医疗保健 （0.2%），
环比增速下降的有生活用品及服务 （-0.1%） 、 教育文化娱乐 （-0.3%） 、 其他用品和服务 （-0.8%），
居住价格环比增速持平；9 月分项中较 8 月环比增速上升的有衣着、医疗保健、生活用品及服务，
较 8 月环比增速下降的有食品烟酒、教育文化娱乐、交通通信、其他用品和服务，居住环比增速持
平于 8 月。 
9 月 CPI 同比增速略低于万得一致预期。9 月 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，较 8 月上升 0.1 个百分点，核心
CPI 同比增长 1.0%，较8 月持平 ， 服务价格同比增长0.8%，较8 月持平 ， 消费品价格同比增长1.0%，
较 8 月上升 0.2 个百分点。从分项同比看，9 月同比增速增长的是其他用品和服务（5.9%） 、交通通
信（4.3%） 、医疗保健 （2.7%） 、教育文化娱乐 （1.4%） 、衣着 （1.2%） 、生活用品及服务 （0.2%），
同比增速下降的是居住（-0.3%）和食品烟酒（-0.9%）；同比增速较 8 月上升的仅有交通通信，居
住、教育文化娱乐和医疗保健增速持平于 8 月，衣着、食品烟酒、生活用品及服务、其他用品和服
务增速均较 8 月有不同程度下降。 
9 月食品价格分化，牛羊肉价格环比涨幅扩大。从环比看，9 月食品价格环比上涨 0.4%，涨幅与上
月相同，低于季节性水平 0.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.06 个百分点；鲜菜、猪肉、鸡蛋和
鲜果价格涨幅在 0.5%-2.3%之间，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.05 个百分点；牛肉和羊肉价格持续上
涨，本月分别上涨 3.1%和 1.4%，涨幅均有扩大，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点；休渔期
结束，水产品供应充足，价格下降 0.8%，影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.02 个百分点。从同比看，食品价格
同比下降 1.7%，降幅比上月扩大 0.3 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点；食品中，猪肉
和鲜菜价格分别下降 10.0%和 8.1%，合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点；粮食、食用油、奶类
和鲜果价格降幅在 0.6%-1.5%之间 ， 合计影响CPI 同比下降约 0.07 个百分点 ； 鸡蛋价格上涨17.4%，
涨幅回落 1.1 个百分点 ， 影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点 ； 牛肉和羊肉价格分别上涨 7.5%和 6.4%，
涨幅继续扩大，合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.08 个百分点。 
服务价格和黄金价格环比回落 ，AI 产品价格同比涨幅扩大。从环比看 ，9 月服务价格由上月上涨 0.1%
转为下降 0.2%，对CPI 环比的影响由上月上拉约 0.04 个百分点转为下拉约 0.08 个百分点 ， 服务中，
随着暑假结束，飞机票、宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格合计影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.12 个百分点；秋季
开学，教育服务价格影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.03 个百分点；工业消费品价格影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.22
个百分点，其中，受国际市场价格变动影响，国内汽油价格和黄金饰品价格两项合计影响 CPI 环比
上涨约 0.14 个百分点；受成本走高影响，移动电话机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑价格合计
影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点 ； 秋装上市 ， 带动服装价格影响CPI 环比上涨约 0.03 个百分点。
从同比看，9 月汽油价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点；扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响 CPI
同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ； 其中 ， 黄金饰品价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.11 个百分点 ， 数据存储设备、
平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.23 个百分点；服务价格涨幅与上月
相同，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点。 
AI 产品价格带动 9 月 CPI 同比增速上行。从同比看，9 月 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，其中，食品价格影
响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点 ， 汽油价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点 ， 扣除能源的工业消
费品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点 ； 从细项
看，猪肉和鲜菜价格合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点，牛肉和羊肉价格合计影响 CPI 同比上
涨约 0.08 个百分点 ， 鸡蛋价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点 ， 黄金饰品价格影响CPI 同比上涨
约 0.11 个百分点 ， 数据存储设备 、 平板电脑 、 计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.23
个百分点。一方面，9 月 CPI 有三个特点与此前是一致的，一是食品价格的持续拖累，二是国际能
源价格和黄金价格的持续扰动，三是内需的疲弱反映在服务价格上；另一方面， 9 月也出现了新的
特点 ， 最为明显的是AI 产品需求带动的价格上升是 9 月 CPI 同比增速较 8 月上升的重要因素 ， 体现
在通信工具价格 9 月同比增长 14.3%、交通通信项目 9 月同比增长 4.3%，其次是以牛肉和羊肉为代
表的食品价格，出现环比走势强于季节性的趋势。我们认为四季度 CPI 同比增速或仍将维持相对平
稳的走势，但是需要开始关注 AI 产品需求对价格的推动、极端天气对食品价格的影响、国际局势不
确定性对大宗商品价格波动的影响，以及 PPI 同比增速上升对 CPI 的外溢性影响。

### Page 4

2026 年 10 月 14 日 9 月通胀点评 4

PPI 
PPI 环比大幅走高。9 月 PPI 环比增长 1.2%，较8 月上升 0.8 个百分点 ， 其中生产资料环比增长1.4%，
生活资料环比增长 0.1%。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，较 8 月上升 1.2 个百分点，其中生产资料同比增
长 6.5%，较 8 月上升 1.5 个百分点，生活资料同比下降 0.1%，较 8 月上升 0.4 个百分点。 
9 月 PPIRM 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 6.9%，较 8 月上升 1.1 个百分点；从构成来看，9 月同比增速
上升较多的是有色金属材料类（ 20.0%），燃料、动力类（ 13.6%）和化工原料类（ 12.6%），同比
增速下降的仅有建筑材料类（-2.3%），农副产品类（-0.4%）和黑色金属材料类（-0.4%）；所有品
类中，同比增速较 8 月上升较多的是燃料、动力类，化工原料类和建筑材料类，同比增速下降的仅
有黑色金属材料类。 
9 月主要行业中出厂价格同比增速较高的是，煤炭开采和洗选业（ 35.3%），石油和天然气开采业
（24.5%）和石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业（21.6%），同比增速较低的是酒、饮料和精制茶制造业
（-5.3%），电力、热力的生产和供应业（-3.9%）和黑色金属矿采选业（-3.4%）；同比增速较 8 月
上升较多的是石油和天然气开采业，石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业和煤炭开采和洗选业，下降的仅
有黑色金属矿采选业，造纸及纸制品业，电力、热力的生产和供应业以及木材加工及木、竹、藤、
棕、草制品业。 
输入性因素和季节性因素对 PPI 同比增速影响较大。9 月 PPI 环比运行的主要特点 ：一是国际原油、
有色金属价格上行带动国内相关行业价格上涨 。 石油开采 、 精炼石油产品制造 、 有机化学原料制造、
有色金属矿采选业 、 有色金属冶炼和压延加工业 ，5 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.63 个百分点。
二是部分能源和原材料行业供需状况变化拉动价格上涨。受冬季供暖备煤需求增加、主产矿区供应
阶段性偏紧、国际煤价上涨预期效应等因素影响，煤炭开采和洗选业、煤炭加工价格环比上涨，钢
材、水泥等建筑材料需求有所回升，加之原材料成本上涨，非金属矿物制品业价格在环比连降 7 个
月后转为上涨，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业价格环比上涨，4 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.38
个百分点。三是人工智能、算电协同等领域加快发展带动部分行业 需求增加、价格上涨。电气机械
和器材制造业 、 计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业2 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.07 个百分点。
从同比看，价格上涨的行业中，煤炭开采和洗选业、石油和天然气开采业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加
工业、化学原料和化学制品制造业、有色金属矿采选业、有色金属冶炼和压延加工业、电气机械和
器材制造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业，8 个行业涨幅比上月均扩大，合计影响 PPI 同比
上涨约 5.42 个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 1.12 个百分点，价格下降的行业中，酒饮料和精制茶制
造业、电力热力生产和供应业、医药制造 业、非金属矿物制品业、汽车制造业， 5 个行业合计下拉
PPI 同比约 0.66 个百分点。 
关注年底补库存对 PPI 的影响。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，涨幅明显高于万得一致预期 4.3%，影响较
大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业价格，从行业看主要 的上行拉动，一是石油、有色、化
工等受输入性因素影响较大的行业 ，二是受AI 需求带动较大的计算机通信行业 ， 三是受季节性因素
影响较大的行业，比如煤炭。我们认为四季度国际局势的不确定性和国内生产端季节性补库存的具
体情况，都可能对 PPI 价格造成影响，但考虑到目前国内供强虚弱的情况没有明显变化，我们认为
PPI 同比增速短期虽然回落空间不大，但进一步上升的空间也较小 。

本报告准确表述了证券分析师的个人观点。该证券分析师声明，本人未在公司内、外部机构兼任有损本人独立性与客
观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。  
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报
告有关情况 。 如有投资者于未经授权的公众媒体看到或从其他机构获得本研究报告的 ， 请慎重使用所获得的研究报告，
以防止被误导，中银国际证券股份有限公司不对其报告理解和使用承担任何责任。  
评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达 克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

## Document 117: 9月ＦＯＭＣ議事録：タカ派姿勢に滲む「データ依存」の慎重さ ～FRBは年内追加利上げを視野に入れるも、決定は今後の経済指標次第～

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月ＦＯＭＣ議事録：タカ派姿勢に滲む「データ依存」の慎重さ   ～FRBは年内追加利上げを視野に入れるも、決定は今後の経済指標次第～.pdf
Report source: DLRI

### Page 1

FED  Watching

2026 年 10 月 8 日

1/5 
9 月ＦＯＭＣ議事録：タカ派姿勢に滲む「データ依存」の慎重さ 
～FRB は年内追加利上げを視野に入れるも、決定は今後の経済指標次第～ 
 経済調査部 主任エコノミスト  桂畑 誠治  
（℡：050-5474-7493）

要旨  
・9 月FOMCでは全会一致（12 対 0）で 0.25%の利上げを決定。非投票メンバーを含む参加者 
全 19 人が利上げに賛成した。 
・経済・金利見通し（SEP）提出者の大半が年内（10 月または 12 月）の更なる利上げを見込 
んでおり、全体としてタカ派的トーンが際立つ。 
・インフレリスクは上振れ傾向にあるものの、中長期のインフレ予想は 2%目標水準に安定。 
FRBは毎会合のデータ次第で柔軟に判断する「オープンな姿勢」を維持している。 
・今後の見通しとしては、景気や労働市場が安定する中でコアCPIやコアPCEの鈍化が確認され 
れば、予防的な追加利上げが見送られる可能性がある（最終決定は 12 月FOMCまでのデータ次
第） 。

１． 9 月FOMC ：参加者 19 人全員 が利上げ に賛成  
ケビン・ウォーシュFRB議長の新体制下で初めての利上げが決定された 9 月FOMCの議事録が公表 
された。新体制では会合直後の声明文のテキスト量が大幅に削減されたほか、記者会見時間も短
縮され、直接的な情報発信量が絞り込まれている。そうした中で、後日公表される「議事録」の
情報量は引き続き高水準に維持されており、FRBの本音を推し量る最重要ツールとしての重みが増
している。ただし、公表がFOMC終了から 3 週間後となるため、情報の遅効性には注意が必要であ 
る。 
9 月の利上げ決定については、投票権のないメンバーも含めた参加者全員（19 人）が利上げに賛
成した。さらに、経済・金利予測（SEP：議長自身は今回も SEP を提出していない）で示されていた 
通り、SEP を提出した 18 人の参加者のうち大半が「年内（10 月 27-28 日または 12 月 15-16 日）に
更なる利上げが適切となる可能性が高い」と見解を示しており、インフレへの強い警戒感を示すタ 
カ派（金融引き締め寄り）なトーンが際立つものとなった。ただし、委員会として今後の政策判断 
は新たに入手されるデータに依存（Data-dependent）するスタンスを明確に維持している。

２．労働市場は完全雇用水準で安定、インフレには上振れリスク 
委員会は全会一致（12 対 0）で、FF金利誘導目標レンジを 0.25％ポイント引き上げ、3.75％〜

### Page 2

2/5 
4.00％とすることを決定した。労働市場が完全雇用に近い水準で安定している一方で、インフレ 
率が高止まりしていることが背景にある。 
インフレの判断では、参加者は共通して、 「インフレ率は高止まりしており、ここ数ヶ月でイン 
フレ抑制に向けた十分な進展は見られていない」と指摘した。 「原油や精製燃料製品の価格を押し 
上げた継続的な地政学的動向や、急増するAI関連投資がインフレ圧力に寄与している」との認識 
が示された。 
複数の参加者がコア財・コアサービス価格の上振れや、関税の影響に言及したほか、数名の参 
加者がPCE指数の算出手法改定による影響や、3 ヶ月年率換算のインフレ率が年後半にインフレを 
過小評価しやすい傾向に留意すべきだと警鐘を鳴らした。一方で参加者全員が、 「中長期のインフ 
レ予想に関する市場ベースおよび調査ベースの指標が、委員会の 2％目標と一致する水準を維持し 
ている」と判断しており、追加利上げを過度に急ぐ必要性は示されなかった。 
労働市場に関しては、参加者は概ね「労働市場の状況が安定している」と判断し、 「完全雇用に 
近い状態にある」と評価した。 「失業率が現在の水準付近にとどまり、労働市場の状況は引き続き 
安定する」と予想した。一方で、数名の参加者が低水準の採用・解雇率など流動性の低下を指摘 
し、一部の参加者がAI専門職における局所的な賃金上昇圧力に触れた。そのうえで、参加者は概
ね「労働市場に対する上振れリスクと下振れリスクはほぼ均衡している」と評価した。 
経済活動に関しても、参加者は一般に「堅調なペースで拡大している」と評価。 「地政学的動向 
による供給面での悪影響が強まったものの、旺盛な設備投資と底堅い個人消費が経済活動を支え 
ている」との判断が示された。なお、一部の参加者からは、AI関連投資が設備投資と潜在生産性 
を押し上げる一方で中期的に総需要が総供給を上回るリスクや、株高などを背景とする金融環境 
の緩和的状況、高所得層と低・中所得層における消費の二極化構造への懸念が指摘された。 
以上のように、参加者は共通して「労働市場が完全雇用に近く力強さの兆候も見られる一方で 
インフレ率は高止まりしており、経済活動が堅調なペースで拡大していること」を強調した。さ
らに、ほぼすべての参加者が、 「インフレリスクは上振れに傾いているものの、労働市場に対する 
リスクは減少し、現在はほぼ均衡している」と評価した。こうした経済見通しと変化するリスク 
バランスに基づき、全参加者（19 人）がFF金利誘導目標レンジの引き上げが適切であると判断し
た。

3．政策金利の水準感とリスク管理の姿勢 
今回の議論において注目されるのは、利上げの根拠と政策金利水準に対する見解の多様さであ 
る。多くの参加者は、予想以上の需要や供給面での悪影響によりインフレ率が持続的に目標を上 
回り続けることに対する保険（Insurance）を提供するため、誘導目標レンジを高めの軌道とする 
ことがリスク管理上の観点から賢明であると強調した。 
一方で、相当数の参加者はリスク管理上の観点からではなく、自身の標準的な見通し（Modal 
outlooks）に基づいて高めの目標軌道が必要であると見なした。さらに、数名の参加者は中立金 
利の推計値を引き上げたことに触れ、現在の政策金利は「緊縮的ではない、あるいは極めて緩や

かに緊縮的であるに過ぎない」とのタカ派的な認識を示した。これは、今後の引き締め継続を正
当化する材料となる。 
また、バランスシート政策に関しては、数名の参加者が国債市場の円滑な機能を認めつつも、 
将来の市場不全に備えたツールやコミュニケーションの強化を提案したことが確認された。

４．先行きに対する見通し 
今回の会合以降の金融政策の見通しに関して、大半の参加者（SEP提出者 18 人のうち）が年末 
までにFF金利誘導目標レンジを再度引き上げることが適切となる可能性が高いとの見方を示し 
た。ただし、全員が共通のスタンスとしてオープンな姿勢で各会合に臨み、今後の決定は新たに 
入手する情報と、それが経済見通しおよびリスクバランスに与える含意に依存することを強調、
毎会合ごとに判断されることを示し、政策の自由度を維持した。 
今回の議事録は全体として「年内追加利上げ」に前向きなタカ派的トーンが目立ったものの、 
インフレ予想の安定や「毎会合データ次第」というスタンスから、FRBが無理な連続利上げを焦っ 
ているわけではないことが示された。今後の金融政策の焦点を左右するのは、10 月・12 月FOMCま
でに発表される景気、労働市場や、コアCPI、コアPCEの動向である。景気や労働市場の安定が続 
くと予想されるもとで、インフレの鈍化傾向が確認される場合、予防的な追加利上げは見送られ 
る可能性がある（最終決定は今後のデータ次第） 。

Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machine ry; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chi nese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and com mercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as d ata centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over  the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

### Page 3

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  October 2026, No.307

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non -current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of t he time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri  
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared a cross Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can furthe r intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative an alysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downw ard pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change.  
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and Ind ia is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

4 
−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads a nd reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN  and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.  
As part of its Global Adaptation 
Initiative (ND‑GAIN), the University of 
Notre Dame (U.S.) evaluates countries' 
ability to use investment to achieve 
climate‑change adaptation in the form of 
a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social 
readiness. Respectively, these 
components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to 
adaptation investment by private -sector 
companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

and policies to support private-sector adaptation investment has been secured, and 3) whether tangible and 
intangible social infrastructure that promotes investment has been established. 
Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low eco nomic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular  need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.

(Tomohiro Hosoi)

Hungary, Turkey and Kazakhstan will announce interest rate
decisions next week, while Poland releases industrial output,
labour market and retail sales data

Adam Antoniak, Peter Virovacz, Muhammet Mercan and Dmitry
Dolgin
Falling inﬂation has markets increasingly betting on a Turkish rate cut
Poland: Industrial output and labour market
data in focus
September industrial output and labour market data will be
released on Tuesday. Although the European and German
economies show signs of recovery, Polish manufacturing remains
fragile and uneven. Some sectors, including electrical equipment,
machinery and equipment, and other transport equipment, are
performing relatively well. Others, such as textiles, furniture and the
automotive industry, continue to struggle. With the calendar eﬀect
neutral (the number of working days is the same as a year earlier)
and a high comparison base, we forecast low single-digit growth in
industrial production in September. At the same time, we expect PPI

Poland’s labour market remains stable. It is no longer exceptionally
tight, nor showing signs of weakening. Wage growth in the
enterprise sector has moderated and is stabilising at around 6.0%,
no longer exerting signiﬁcant pressure on price growth.
Employment continues to decline amid an ageing population and
an insuﬃcient inﬂow of younger workers to replace those retiring,

particularly in manufacturing. Enterprise-sector data do not
capture the employment of immigrant workers.
Thursday brings the release of September retail sales data. Despite
some moderation, private consumption appears relatively resilient
to the current energy shock and high retail fuel prices. To some
extent, the impact of rising fuel prices on household ﬁnances is
being cushioned by government measures temporarily reducing
excise duty and VAT on fuels. Demand for durable goods remains

solid, while some restraint is visible in day-to-day spending on
necessities. Given the substantial savings accumulated in previous
quarters, households should be able to smooth their consumption
over time. Nevertheless, household consumption growth this year
is projected to be slower than in 2025.
Hungary: NBH expected to keep rates
unchanged
The National Bank of Hungary’s watchlist is fairly limited to ﬁscal
policy and risk sentiment. In practice, however, it is diﬃcult to

assess these factors in the run-up to the October rate-setting
meeting on Tuesday. Although inﬂation ﬁgures were dovish,
market volatility in relation to geopolitical developments remains
high. With no clear de-escalation in sight, risk sentiment won't
allow the NBH to cut rates now.
Regarding ﬁscal policy, although the central bank is aware of the
2027 budget, as the NBH governor sits on the Fiscal Council that
reviews and approves it, no public information will be available on

this. Therefore, we do not believe this will provide an opportunity for
immediate monetary policy easing. Given the timing, we anticipate

Turkey: Central bank expected to cut rates
by 100bp
Thursday brings Turkey's October interest rate decision. Recent
inﬂation data has boosted the likelihood of a rate cut.
In addition, investment fund volatility is likely to increase downside

risks to activity in the last quarter, while we expect the
macroeconomic impact to be manageable.
Given the September CPI reading and domestic demand weakness,
we expect the central bank to cut the policy rate by 100bp in the
October MPC meeting to 36%.
CCA: Kazakhstan expected to deliver
another rate cut
For the 23 October decision in Kazakhstan, a 25bp cut to 16.00% is
now our base case, as it meets the criteria we indicated after the
September rate decision: headline inﬂation slowed by 0.5pp to

9.3% YoY, households’ price expectations improved, and the tenge’s
strength continued to exceed expectations in September.
At the same time, a hold remains plausible, as the recent slowdown
in CPI was overwhelmingly food-driven, while a renewed
acceleration in overall inﬂation, taking it further away from the 5%
long-term target, remains a risk. Importantly, the global backdrop
of rising core rates also argues for keeping real rates high.
Regardless of the decision, we believe the scope for policy easing at

this point is limited.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
 Markets regained some footing to close off the week after 
President Trump said the US would not strike Iran before 
next month’s midterm elections, supporting risk sentiment 
and helping equities recover. However, tensions flared again 
over the weekend, with an attack on Saudi Arabia’s main 
airport and a US strike on a commercial tanker in the Gulf of 
Oman. On Friday, President Trump also announced that Russia 
would supply 13.5 million barrels of diesel to global markets 
through October to help ease fuel shortages and lower prices, 
a move that would mark a significant departure from years 
of sanctions despite the ongoing war in Ukraine. US bond 
markets will be closed on Monday for Columbus Day, although 
equity markets remain open. 
• US equities closed higher on Friday, capping a volatile week 
that saw sharp moves in both bond yields and energy markets. 
The S&P 500 rose 0.6%, although it remained below the 
record high reached earlier in the week. The Dow Jones gained 
0.8%, while the Nasdaq also advanced 0.8%, rebounding 
from Thursday’s tech-led sell-off amid concerns over the pace 
of AI-related growth. European equities outperformed as 
volatility in regional bond markets eased, with the Euro Stoxx 
50 rising 0.8% and the FTSE 100 up 1.1%.
• Asian equities delivered a mixed performance on Friday. The 
Nikkei 225 closed flat, recovering from an intraday decline of 
1.3% after reports that OpenAI’s revenue targets may indeed 
be reached. The CSI 300 was up 0.2%, while the Hang Seng 
rose 1.8%, supported by gains in technology stocks. The ASX 
200 advanced 0.6%, with futures pointing to a further gain at 
today’s open. South Korea’s Kospi was closed for Hangul Day.
• US Treasury yields were little changed as investors digested 
President Trump’s pledge not to take military action against 
Iran until after the midterm elections. The 2-year and 10-
year Treasury rose 3bps and 1bp respectively, with the latter 
holding at 5.24%, below the 24-year high reached earlier in 
the week. Attention now turns to the September CPI report, 
due Wednesday night (11:30pm AEDT), and comments from 
Fed Chair Warsh, both of which are expected to shape market 
expectations ahead of the FOMC’s October meeting. Futures 
currently assign a 17% probability to an October rate hike and 
have almost fully priced in a hike by December, leaving the 
implied year-end policy rate at 4.13%. 
•  European bonds ended lower after a volatile week. The 
10-year bund yield fell 1bp, while the 10-year gilts declined 
4bps. France’s 10-year OAT yield also dropped 4bps, although 
concerns over the government’s ability to reduce the budget 
deficit to 5% of GDP by 2027 continue to weigh on sentiment.
• In Australia, the 3-year government bond yield was 
unchanged at 4.90%, while the 10-year yield fell 6bps to 
5.34%. Attention this week will centre on the September 
Labour Force Survey, due Thursday, where a moderation in 
the participation rate from its near 115-year high is expected 
to keep the unemployment rate broadly steady (see our 
preview). Swap markets continue to assign only a modest 
probability to further policy tightening, with around 26% 
odds of a November rate hike currently priced.
• The US dollar index rose modestly, gaining 0.1%. The AUD 
rose against the greenback, up 0.3% to 0.6978. Other crosses 
against the USD were mixed, with the yen up 0.2%, euro 
losing 0.2% and the pound flat.
• Commodities were mostly higher. Oil prices recovered from 
early losses, with Brent crude rising 0.4% to US$104.7/bbl 
and WTI also gaining 0.4%. Elsewhere, gold advanced 1.5% 
and copper rose 1.6%, while iron ore bucked the trend, falling 
0.7% amid a subdued outlook for demand. 
International Data
In the US, the University of Michigan consumer sentiment 
deteriorated marginally in the preliminary October print, the 
headline index declining from 48.1 to 46.3. Current conditions 
fell from 50.9 to 44.7 while expectations edged up to 47.3. 
Historically low sentiment is yet to stop consumers from 
spending though, with real consumption growth remaining 
robust through 2026.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
NOVEMBER CPI
PREVIEW AND ANALYSIS
Complete Montly CPI will take some time to understand
19 December 2025
Key points
• The October monthly CPI was 
flat in the month, broadly as we 
expected but stronger than market 
expectations. For more information see 
“Electrical noise is far from static”.
• The impact on our inflation profile 
was marginal. The December quarter 
CPI remains 0.6%qtr/3.6%yr, while 
the Trimmed Mean edged up from 
0.7%qtr/3.1%yr to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr.
• Our November monthly CPI estimate 
is 0.4% for the month, leaving the 
annual pace unchanged at 3.8%yr.
• The introduction of the complete 
monthly CPI is a significant enhancement 
to our analysis of price formation in 
the Australian economy. However, 
with many series having only a short 
history, it will take time to fully 
understand some of the finer details.
• For now, we will continue to place 
greater emphasis on the quarterly 
measures of core inflation.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
No surprise in the October CPI
The new complete monthly CPI gained 3.8% in the year to 
October, coming in a touch softer than Westpac’s estimate of 
3.9%yr but a bit stronger than the market estimate of 3.6%yr. 
So while the market and other commentators may have been 
surprised by the result it was broadly within our expectations 
and as such has had just a marginal impact on our inflation 
profile.
The October CPI was flat in the month, a touch softer than 
Westpac’s published near-cast of 0.1% on the back of a larger 
than expected decline in electricity (–10.2%) which was only 
partially offset by a smaller than expected decline in auto fuel 
(–0.9%) and an unexpected bump up in garments (+2.6%).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) lifted 0.3% in the month of October, 
the same monthly increase it has seen for the previous two 
months and down from the 0.5%mth increase in July but 
stronger than the 0.2%mth prints from March to June.
This saw the annual pace of the TM increase to 3.3% from 
3.2% in September. This month the TM excluded the annual 
rise in electricity; alongside other large price rises and falls. 
Due to the short history of many series in the monthly CPI, the 
annual pace of monthly TM inflation can only be calculated 
back to April 2025. Before then the ABS notes that the annual 
pace is calculated from the quarterly CPI data.
For more information on the October monthly CPI see 
“Electrical noise is far from static ”.
Monthly CPI points to some momentum
Following the October CPI we have seen some minor revisions 
to the various components in our December quarter CPI 
estimate with the upward revisions, such as transport via auto 
fuel and motor vehicles, being offset by downward revisions 
to recreation, alcohol & tobacco and utilities in particular. This 
has left our December CPI estimate unchanged at 0.6%qtr.   
As our Chief Economist, Luci Ellis, has noted that inflation 
did see a bump in the September quarter and again in the 
month of October. However, the main sources of the surprise 
have had little to do with domestic demand or labour market 
pressures. Rather, a sizeable part of the bump looks to have 
been from administered prices and noise. Since the release of 
the data, the RBA has recognised this issue but at the post 
RBA Board Decision Media Conference in December, the 
Governor communicated that the Bank is more worried about 
upside risks to inflation than first thought.
We agree that some of this momentum will continue into the 
December quarter as highlighted by our upward revision in our 
We will continue to focus on the quarterly prints
Justin Smirk
Senior Economist

### Page 3

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
December quarter TM estimate to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr from our 
earlier estimate of 0.7%qtr/3.1%yr. 
For the December quarter, price changes that are included in 
the TM go from a low of –0.26% to a high of 1.66%. Readers 
will remember that the TM is calculated from seasonally 
adjusted price changes and not the original estimates so if 
unaffected by regular seasonal price shifts. We estimate 
that the most significant components to be trimmed off 
the top in the quarter are: electricity (2.7%); other services 
for motor vehicles (2.5%); tobacco (2.1%); and automotive 
fuel (1.7%). Trimmed off the bottom are: other non-durable 
household products (–0.39%); garments for women (–0.92%); 
international holiday travel (–0.97%); vegetables (–1.16%) and 
wine (–1.27%).   
However, we do not expect this momentum to continue 
through 2026. As Luci noted in her article “ RBA on hold 
in 2026, risks on both sides ” we expect inflation to keep 
easing, due to the lagged impact of past restrictive policy 
and the unwind of earlier government support measures. We 
believe that many commentators have overlooked these lags. 
Monetary policy typically takes a year or more to fully affect 
jobs and prices, so current outcomes reflect the peak cash rate 
from a year ago and we expect to see further labour market 
softening. Strong hiring across the care economy has masked 
some of that restrictiveness, as has public infrastructure 
spending, but these supports are now fading along with last 
year’s tax-cut boost. We believe that even with the 75bps in  
rate cuts this year, policy remains mildly restrictive, and given 
usual lags, this will keep showing in the data through 2026.
Taking a look at some of the recent larger moves in monthly 
prices can highlight many of the issues Luci raises. Taking the 
seasonally adjusted data, smoothing it with a three month 
average then calculating the change over three months (so we 
have three months on three months percentage change) we 
find at the top end of the list some price increases you would 
not expect to continue at the same pace. 
For example at the top are: lamb & goat (8.1%, highly 
seasonal); accessories (8.1% with jewellery prices surging 
on the back of record high gold prices); audio visual & 
computing services (6.0% due to Netflix subscriptions); beef 
& veal (3.7%, highly seasonal); coffee & tea (3.6% noting 
global coffee prices have passed their peak and are already 
significantly lower); water & sewerage (2.3% IPART decision 
was for a 13.8% increase for Sydney Water in October); 
property rates & charges (2.3% administered prices); and 
electricity (1.2%, reflecting unwinding of cost of living 
rebates). Offsetting some of these increases was falling prices 
for: men’s footwear (–2.0%); vegetables (–2.0% seasonal); 
eggs (–1.5%, reflecting the unwinding of the price spike 
associated with an outbreak of bird flu); and major household 
appliances (–1.2%). 
Core inflation still to moderate through 2026
We are projecting headline inflation to get back to the RBA’s 
target band, but not until later in 2026. We now see core 
inflation back below the midpoint of the 2–3% range at the 
end of the year. We had thought this might be achieved by the 
third quarter but now expect current momentum to continue
